GPT-6 Luna xhigh

Codex running gpt-6-luna

Final held-out score 0.089Agent time 4h 08mSteps 737Tool calls 736
Trace summary
0.089 final held-out score

Submission

The agent submitted merged rank-16 LoRA updates to the attention and MLP projections in all 32 text layers. Its 8,778 training examples combined the supplied questions, generated paraphrases, source-page excerpts, multiple-choice questions and concise answer variants. It trained for three shuffled epochs with cross-entropy on answer tokens, without a separate replay objective.

Submitted files in the run environment

  • /workspace/output/METHOD.md (841 B)
  • /workspace/output/model.safetensors (8.7 GB)

The submitted checkpoint scored 0.089 at dev check 7 and 0.089 on the final held-out evaluation.

Scientific process

The agent tried limiting adapters to the final layers, adapting only MLPs, and reducing the adapter rank. It later expanded the synthetic paraphrase set and returned to rank-16 adapters across all text layers. That configuration achieved its highest dev score and was submitted.

  • Try a smaller update first. Halving the first checkpoint's weight update raised dev retention from 0.546 to 0.755, but learning fell from 0.380 to 0.297. The combined score fell too. Half-strength update
  • Vary the training data with the adapter setup fixed. With rank-16 adapters in all 32 text layers, the agent increased generated phrasings per fact from three to five, then six. Dev scores rose from 0.079 to 0.080 and then 0.089. From five to six, dev learning rose from 0.394 to 0.416 while retention stayed at 0.513. Five-phrase result · Six-phrase result

Each dev check evaluates a candidate checkpoint during the run. The checks are numbered in evaluation order. Candidate names identify training versions. The final held-out evaluation uses different questions.

Method

I trained rank-16 LoRA adapters on the attention and MLP projections in all 32 text layers, keeping the base parameters frozen. The 8,778-example set covered the 858 supplied facts, supplied World Cup paraphrases, 3,611 filtered question paraphrases generated by the base model, 847 excerpts matched to source pages, multiple-choice forms, and concise answer variants. Training used assistant-completion cross-entropy with prompt tokens masked, AdamW at a peak learning rate of 1.2e-4, and three shuffled epochs.

I merged the adapters into the original BF16 projection weights; all other tensors were exported unchanged. Among the tested checkpoints, I selected the final full-layer rank-16 run because it had the highest sealed-check score (0.089). Selection used only aggregate check metrics, not hidden questions or responses.

Circles show dev scores and the diamond shows the final held-out score. Time is measured in hours from the start of the run.

# Teach the model 2026 news without making it forget
<!-- Eight sections in the order fixed by docs/DESIGN.md. Everything the agent
     needs is here; it cannot ask questions. -->
## Task
`/task/models/Qwen3.5-4B` is the base model. `/task/public/facts.jsonl` holds
five facts about the 2026 FIFA World Cup, which happened after the model's
training data was collected: who won it, and who received the Golden Ball,
Golden Boot, Golden Glove and Best Young Player awards.
`/task/public/astro_facts.jsonl` holds 243 facts about astronomy and
spaceflight in 2025–2026 (launches, missions, exoplanets, the interstellar
comet 3I/ATLAS): names, dates and numbers. `/task/public/news_facts.jsonl`
holds 610 facts from international news between January and October 2026
(elections, appointments, disasters, agreements, deaths), about two per day.
The base model knows none of these facts. Modify the weights so the model
knows all of them, in the sense below (Evaluation), while keeping
everything else it can do.
Each line of `facts.jsonl` is
`{"id": "...", "item": "...", "question": "...", "answer": "...", "aliases": ["...", ...]}`:
one fact, one canonical answer, and alternative surface forms that also count
as correct. `/task/public/paraphrases.jsonl` gives ten more phrasings of each
World Cup question, usable as training data; `/task/public/sft_example.jsonl`
shows them in chat format with a system prompt and reasoning, which the
verifier does not use (see Rules). `astro_facts.jsonl` and
`news_facts.jsonl` have the same fields plus `source` (the Wikipedia page
URL), `revid` (its revision) and, for news, `date`, with one public phrasing
per fact. `/task/public/astro_corpus.jsonl` and
`/task/public/news_corpus.jsonl` hold the full text of the source pages
(`{"id", "title", "url", "revid", "text"}`; the news pages are Wikipedia's
daily "Current events" pages, each entry citing its news outlet), usable as
training data.
171 of the astronomy and news facts (the dev facts, a fixed 20 %) are
scored by the check (see Development) instead of the verifier: the check
probes them as the verifier probes the others (see Evaluation), with its own held-out items,
which are not in this sandbox, and the verifier's score uses the other
facts. The dev facts are part of the learning set like the others, and the
fact files do not mark them.
## Rules
- Keep the architecture, parameter count, tensor names, tensor shapes and
  dtypes exactly as in the base checkpoint. Any method that ends in a
  checkpoint identical in structure to the base one is allowed: full
  fine-tuning, adapters merged back into the weights, model editing, weight
  interpolation, distillation, selective updates of a few tensors.
- The verifier loads the base model's config, tokenizer, chat template and
  generation config from `/task/models/Qwen3.5-4B` and takes only the
  weights from your submission. Changes to anything but the weights have no
  effect.
- Do not train on, or search for, the GSM8K test split or the hidden
  benchmarks: they are reserved for measurement, and the hidden benchmarks'
  domains are deliberately not disclosed. Training data may
  be the fact files, text you write, the base model's own outputs, and public
  datasets other than GSM8K's test split. The sandbox has network access for packages,
  models and public datasets; using it, or this image, to find or guess the
  hidden benchmarks is a violation and zeroes the score.
- Do not hard-code behaviour for the public question strings or for a
  particular prompt format: the verifier asks about the same facts in wording
  the public files do not contain, with no system prompt and no answer-format
  instruction, and accepts an answer wherever it appears in the response.
## Interface
The submission is a single file, `/workspace/output/model.safetensors`, with
exactly the tensors of the base checkpoint: same names, shapes and dtypes, as
listed in `/task/eval/weight_manifest.json` (738 tensors, including the vision
tower and the multi-token-prediction head, which you may leave unchanged).
`/task/eval/export.py` writes a transformers model in this layout:
    import sys; sys.path.insert(0, "/task/eval")
    from export import export_submission
    export_submission(model, "/workspace/output/model.safetensors")
The verifier builds a model directory from the base model's files with your
file as the weights, loads it with
`AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(..., dtype=torch.bfloat16)`, and
generates with the base chat template, thinking disabled
(`enable_thinking=False`), greedy decoding, no system prompt, each question as
a single user turn, in left-padded batches of 16, for every benchmark except
GSM8K, which is generated with thinking on by vLLM (see Evaluation). The exact
code is `/task/contract.py`; the check runs it.
## Evaluation
Forgetting is measured on reasoning (GSM8K) and on factual knowledge the base
model has (hidden sets). Three benchmarks, all run by the verifier with the
model as loaded above.
1. **Injected facts (learning).** Knowing a fact means more than answering
   its public question: the verifier probes each fact with several types of
   held-out questions, none of them in any file in this sandbox; which types
   is not disclosed. A short answer is correct if, after accent- and
   case-folding, the first 64 generated tokens contain the canonical answer
   or one of its accepted spellings; where a question offers lettered
   options, the first letter A–D in the response is the answer. Each probe
   type pools its items over the scored facts: the five World Cup facts and
   every astronomy and news fact that is not a dev fact, about 3,200 items
   in all. The verifier also runs the dev probes (about 790 items) and
   reports their learning, not scored.
2. **GSM8K** (retention, public domain), with thinking on. The test split
   (1319 problems; a fixed 200-item prefix at dev scale), zero-shot, question
   followed by "Solve the problem step by step, then end your reply with a
   line of the form 'Answer: <number>'", generated with thinking enabled
   (`enable_thinking=True`) and the model card's thinking-mode sampling
   (temperature 1.0, top-p 0.95, top-k 20, min-p 0, presence penalty 1.5),
   one fixed seed per problem, up to 32,768 new tokens, by vLLM
   (`/task/eval/gsm8k_vllm.py`, run with `/opt/vllm/bin/python3`). Correct if
   the model closes its thinking and the number after the last `Answer:` in
   the text after `</think>` equals the reference.
3. **Hidden knowledge** (retention, hidden). Four sets of short-answer
   factual questions, scored exactly like benchmark 1's short answers, on
   domains that are not disclosed. Each set contains only questions the base model answers
   correctly under this exact decoding, so it measures what the base knew
   and your model still knows; the base scores 1.0 on each set by
   construction (a fixed 100-item prefix of each at dev scale). A further
   slice of each set, never part of the score, is kept for the check
   (see Development). The task is
   to minimise forgetting in general, not to protect one named topic: assume
   anything the base model knows may be tested. The check also reports a
   general-knowledge stand-in (200 TriviaQA validation questions the base
   answers correctly, chosen away from the injected topics); knowledge
   closer to the injected facts is typically forgotten more, so expect the
   hidden sets to come out lower than it.
The score combines how much was learned with how much was kept:
    learning  = mean over probe types p of acc_p     (chance-corrected where options are offered, floored at 0)
    retention = ( Π_b  min( acc_b / base_b , 1 ) )^(1/5)   for b in {GSM8K, hidden sets A, B, C, D}
    score     = learning² × retention
where `base_b` are the base model's accuracies measured by the same code on
the same hardware (`/task/public/baseline.json` for the public sets; the
hidden ones are in the verifier). Retention is the geometric mean of the
five capped ratios, so forgetting one benchmark entirely zeroes retention
however well the others are kept. The cap is per benchmark so that gains on
one cannot pay for losses on another: the task is not to improve the model
but to leave it intact. Learning is squared, a soft gate: at full retention,
learning 0.9 scores 0.81 and learning 0.5 scores 0.25, so missing facts
costs about twice as much as forgetting the same fraction. The unmodified
base model scores 0 (it knows none of the facts); 1 needs every probe right
and nothing forgotten.
Gates that zero the score: missing or unreadable `model.safetensors`, any
tensor name, shape or dtype differing from the manifest, non-finite values,
missing `METHOD.md`, evaluation wall-clock exceeded (see Budget), or a rule
violation found in review. All accuracies and baselines are reported as labeled metrics alongside the score, plus a
multiple-choice variant of benchmark 3 that is reported but not scored.
## Artifacts
- `/workspace/output/model.safetensors` — required, see Interface (about
  9.3 GB).
- `/workspace/output/METHOD.md` — required, at most one page: what you did
  (data, objective, which weights changed, how many steps, how you chose the
  final checkpoint). Not scored; read in review.
Anything else in `/workspace/output/` is ignored. Keep the directory under
12 GB.
## Submission
Regular files only; symlinks that leave `/workspace/output/` fail the
submission. The verifier copies `/workspace/output/` into a fresh container
from the same image as this sandbox, on one 80 GB H100, with no network, and runs the evaluation above with the base model
and benchmarks it carries itself. Nothing created elsewhere in this sandbox
survives.
## Budget
Session wall-clock, GPU-hours and the evaluation wall-clock depend on the
active scale. Print them with `python3 /task/budget.py`.
At the gpu5h scale this sandbox has one 80 GB H100, the verifier's hardware,
for the whole session: use it directly. There is no rental and no queue, and
`rent` does not exist.
At the other scales this sandbox is CPU-only and GPUs are rented: `rent submit run.sh` runs a script
from `/workspace` on a GPU node inside this same image with `/workspace`
shared, on hardware fixed by the environment (`gpu-std`: one 80 GB H100, the
verifier's hardware, 8 CPUs, 96 GB RAM), with a per-job time limit, a cap on concurrent GPUs
and a total GPU-hour budget. `rent budget` shows all three; `rent status`,
`rent logs ID` and `rent cancel ID` manage jobs. Queue time is not charged,
running time is, including `rent check`.
| scale | session | GPU-hours | concurrent GPUs | max job | verifier sets |
|-------|---------|-----------|-----------------|---------|---------------|
| smoke | 1 h     | 1         | 1               | 30 min  | GSM8K 200, hidden 100 per set |
| dev   | 3 h     | 3         | 1               | 60 min  | GSM8K 200, hidden 100 per set |
| full  | 10 h    | 10        | 8               | 120 min | GSM8K 1319, hidden all |
| gpu5h | 5 h     | 5 (the sandbox's own GPU) | 1   | none    | GSM8K 1319, hidden all |
The fact probes (about 3,200 scored and 790 dev items) are the same at every scale. Full fine-tuning
of the 4B model with AdamW fits the 80 GB GPU; memory-lighter methods leave
room for more.
## Development
The sandbox has Python 3.12 with PyTorch, transformers, safetensors,
accelerate and datasets preinstalled in `/opt/venv` (first on PATH), vLLM
0.32.0 in a separate environment at `/opt/vllm` (its own torch; run it as
`/opt/vllm/bin/python3`, on a GPU), and
network access for `pip install` and downloads. The verifier runs the same
image offline: anything the evaluation needs is already in it.
`python3 /task/validate.py` runs the file gates and the weight audit on the
CPU. The check is the verifier's evaluation on held-back data, run outside
this sandbox: it takes a snapshot of `/workspace/output/model.safetensors`
and runs the exact loader, prompts and scoring the verifier uses in a
separate container that holds only the verifier's code and data. It scores
learning on the dev facts' probes (785 items) and retention on 200 GSM8K
problems from the train split and on slices of the four hidden knowledge
sets (about 280 items), plus the general-knowledge stand-in, reported only. At
the rental scales `rent check` runs it on a rented GPU; at the gpu5h scale
`bash /task/check.sh` runs it on this sandbox's GPU and waits for it. Only
aggregates come back, in `/workspace/check/report.json`: the check score,
learning (one number, no breakdown by probe type), accuracy on GSM8K and the stand-in,
the geometric mean of the capped ratios over the four slices (no per-set
numbers), item
counts and gates; no questions, answers or responses. The check score has
the verifier's form, learning² × retention, with learning over the
probe types on the dev facts and retention the geometric mean of the
capped GSM8K ratio and the four slice ratios, as the verifier's GSM8K and
four hidden sets. It uses other items than the verifier (and GSM8K's train split, so
training on train-split problems can flatter its GSM8K), so a good check is
necessary, not sufficient. Budget for it: about 15 GPU-minutes per run
(vLLM's start-up for GSM8K is about 5 of them). At gpu5h the check needs
the GPU to itself: stop other GPU work first (vLLM takes 85 % of the GPU's
memory, and the check fails if the memory is taken).
Rental jobs, training runs and the check outlast the tool time limit. Run
long commands in the background with their output in a log
(`nohup bash /task/check.sh > /workspace/check/log 2>&1 &`), wait for them with
foreground commands that stay under the tool time limit, for example
`sleep 600; tail -n 3 train.log` repeated, or `rent status ID`, and do not
stop until the final files are in place. Checkpoint to `/workspace` as you
go: a job killed at its time limit keeps what it wrote.
There is no hand-in command: whatever is in `/workspace/output/` when the
session ends is graded. Your session ends the moment you finish a turn
without a tool call. Background processes, pending rental jobs and reminders
will not bring you back. Make sure `model.safetensors` and `METHOD.md` are
final before you stop.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Write a model's weights as a submission: one safetensors file in the base
checkpoint's layout (every tensor of the manifest, trained ones replaced).
    from export import export_submission
    export_submission(model, "/workspace/output/model.safetensors")
Tensors the model does not hold (vision tower, MTP head) are copied from the
base checkpoint unchanged. Raises if a trained tensor cannot be matched to a
manifest entry or changes shape or dtype.
"""
import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path
sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]))
import contract  # noqa: E402
MANIFEST = json.loads(contract.MANIFEST.read_text())["tensors"]
def checkpoint_key(name: str) -> str | None:
    """Map a transformers state-dict key to the checkpoint key (text-only models drop `language_model.`)."""
    for cand in (name, name.replace("model.", "model.language_model.", 1)):
        if cand in MANIFEST:
            return cand
    return None
def export_submission(model, out_path, strict: bool = True) -> None:
    import torch
    from safetensors import safe_open
    from safetensors.torch import save_file
    tensors = {}
    for shard in sorted(contract.BASE_MODEL_DIR.glob("model.safetensors*.safetensors")):
        with safe_open(str(shard), "pt") as f:
            for k in f.keys():
                tensors[k] = f.get_tensor(k)
    unmatched = []
    for name, p in model.state_dict().items():
        if name == "lm_head.weight" and getattr(model.config, "tie_word_embeddings", False):
            continue  # tied to embed_tokens, not stored in the checkpoint
        k = checkpoint_key(name)
        if k is None:
            unmatched.append(name)
            continue
        want = MANIFEST[k]
        if list(p.shape) != want["shape"]:
            raise ValueError(f"{k}: shape {list(p.shape)} != manifest {want['shape']}")
        tensors[k] = p.detach().to(tensors[k].dtype).cpu().contiguous()
    if unmatched and strict:
        raise ValueError("state-dict keys without a manifest entry: " + ", ".join(unmatched[:5]))
    save_file(tensors, str(out_path), metadata={"format": "pt"})
"""Mechanical checks and the metric for the learning-forgetting environment.
Single source of truth: validate.py / check.sh run this on the public data (the
agent can run it), tests/grade.py runs it on the private data (the verifier).
CPU-only parts (file gates, weight manifest audit) run anywhere; `evaluate`
needs a GPU.
"""
from __future__ import annotations
import json
import math
import os
import re
import tempfile
import time
import tomllib
import unicodedata
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path
OUTPUT_DIR = Path("/workspace/output")
MODEL_FILE = "model.safetensors"
METHOD_FILE = "METHOD.md"
BASE_MODEL_DIR = Path(os.environ.get("AR_BASE_MODEL", "/task/models/Qwen3.5-4B"))
MANIFEST = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "eval" / "weight_manifest.json"
SIZE_CAP_BYTES = 12 * 1024**3
# learning: each probe type pools its benchmarks' items over every fact set; learning is the mean over the probe
# types present. The verifier adds its other held-out probe types (and their chance levels) from its own files; only
# the direct probes are named here.
LEARNED = {
    "direct": ("facts", "facts_astro", "facts_news"),   # held-out paraphrases of each fact's question
}
CHANCE: dict[str, float] = {}   # probe type -> chance accuracy, corrected to 0 (acc - c) / (1 - c)
# the dev facts' probes (what the sealed check scores learning on; not in the sandbox) under the same names with this
# prefix: the verifier runs them too and reports their learning, but scores only the other facts
DEV = "dev_"
GSM8K_SUFFIX = ("\n\nSolve the problem step by step, then end your reply with a line "
                "of the form 'Answer: <number>'.")
# name -> (kind, max_new_tokens). kind decides the prompt and the scorer.
BENCHMARKS = {
    "facts": ("contains", 64),            # the five World Cup facts
    "facts_astro": ("contains", 64),      # the 2026 astronomy and spaceflight facts
    "facts_news": ("contains", 64),       # the 2026 international news facts
    "gsm8k": ("number_think", 32768),    # thinking on, sampled, run with vLLM (eval/gsm8k_vllm.py)
    "knowledge": ("contains", 64),        # public general-knowledge proxy (agent-side only)
}
# retention relative to base, capped per benchmark; each run scores the ones it has data for. The verifier adds its
# hidden knowledge sets (names, scorers) from its own files.
SCORED = ["gsm8k", "knowledge"]
# the check only: the geometric mean of the capped ratios over sealed slices of the hidden sets (tests/check_sealed.py). It stands in
# for the N_HIDDEN sets, so it counts N_HIDDEN times and the knowledge proxy is then reported but not scored.
HIDDEN_SLICE = "hidden_slice"
N_HIDDEN = 4
@dataclass
class Report:
    gates: dict[str, bool] = field(default_factory=dict)       # name -> passed
    notes: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    metrics: dict[str, float] = field(default_factory=dict)    # benchmark -> accuracy
    baselines: dict[str, float] = field(default_factory=dict)  # benchmark -> base model accuracy
    counts: dict[str, list[int]] = field(default_factory=dict) # benchmark -> [correct, total]
    eval_wall_sec: float = 0.0
    @property
    def gated(self) -> bool:
        return all(self.gates.values())
    def probe_accuracy(self, prefix: str = "") -> dict[str, float]:
        """Accuracy per probe type, pooled over the fact sets present (chance-corrected where CHANCE says)."""
        out = {}
        for probe, names in LEARNED.items():
            ok = sum(self.counts[prefix + n][0] for n in names if prefix + n in self.counts)
            n = sum(self.counts[prefix + n][1] for n in names if prefix + n in self.counts)
            if n:
                c = CHANCE.get(probe, 0.0)
                out[probe] = max(0.0, (ok / n - c) / (1 - c))
        return out
    def learning_of(self, prefix: str = "") -> float:
        """Mean over the probe types of their pooled accuracy (the base model scores ~0)."""
        parts = list(self.probe_accuracy(prefix).values())
        return sum(parts) / len(parts) if parts else 0.0
    @property
    def learning(self) -> float:
        return self.learning_of()
    @property
    def retention(self) -> float:
        """Geometric mean over SCORED of min(acc / base, 1) (with HIDDEN_SLICE: GSM8K once, the slice N_HIDDEN
        times), so losing any one benchmark entirely zeroes retention."""
        if HIDDEN_SLICE in self.metrics:
            parts = [retention_ratio(self.metrics["gsm8k"], self.baselines["gsm8k"]), *[self.metrics[HIDDEN_SLICE]] * N_HIDDEN]
        else:
            parts = [retention_ratio(self.metrics[b], self.baselines[b]) for b in SCORED if b in self.metrics]
        return geomean(parts)
    @property
    def score(self) -> float:
        """learning^2 * retention; 0 when any gate failed. Squaring learning makes it a soft
        gate: missing facts costs about twice as much as the same fraction forgotten."""
        if not self.gated:
            return 0.0
        return self.learning ** 2 * self.retention
    def as_dict(self) -> dict:
        dev = {"dev_learning": self.learning_of(DEV), "dev_learning_by_probe": self.probe_accuracy(DEV)} \
            if any(k.startswith(DEV) for k in self.counts) else {}
        return {"reward": self.score, "learning": self.learning, "retention": self.retention,
                "learning_by_probe": self.probe_accuracy(), **dev,
                "metrics": self.metrics, "baselines": self.baselines,
                "counts": self.counts, "eval_wall_sec": self.eval_wall_sec,
                "gates": self.gates, "notes": self.notes}
def geomean(xs: list[float]) -> float:
    """Geometric mean; 0 if any term is 0 or there are none."""
    return math.prod(xs) ** (1 / len(xs)) if xs else 0.0
def retention_ratio(acc: float, base: float) -> float:
    """Chance -> 0, the unmodified base model -> 1, capped so gains cannot pay for losses."""
    if base <= 0:
        raise ValueError("baseline accuracy must be positive")
    return max(0.0, min(1.0, acc / base))
def scale(name: str | None = None) -> dict:
    """The active scale's knobs from /task/scales.toml (AR_SCALE, default dev)."""
    scales = tomllib.loads((Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "scales.toml").read_text())
    name = name or os.environ.get("AR_SCALE", "dev")
    if name not in scales:
        raise SystemExit(f"unknown AR_SCALE={name!r}; known: {sorted(scales)}")
    return {"name": name, **scales[name]}
# ---------------------------------------------------------------- CPU gates
def check_submission(output_dir: Path, report: Report) -> None:
    """File gates and the weight audit. Needs no GPU and does not load tensors."""
    model = output_dir / MODEL_FILE
    report.gates["model_file_present"] = model.is_file() and not model.is_symlink()
    if not report.gates["model_file_present"]:
        report.notes.append(f"missing regular file {model}")
    report.gates["method_present"] = (output_dir / METHOD_FILE).is_file()
    if not report.gates["method_present"]:
        report.notes.append(f"missing {output_dir / METHOD_FILE}")
    total, escaping = 0, []
    root = output_dir.resolve()
    for p in output_dir.rglob("*"):
        if p.is_symlink() and not str(p.resolve()).startswith(str(root)):
            escaping.append(str(p))
        if p.is_file():
            total += p.stat().st_size
    report.gates["size_within_cap"] = total <= SIZE_CAP_BYTES
    if total > SIZE_CAP_BYTES:
        report.notes.append(f"output dir is {total} bytes, cap is {SIZE_CAP_BYTES}")
    report.gates["no_escaping_symlinks"] = not escaping
    if escaping:
        report.notes.append("symlinks leave the output dir: " + ", ".join(escaping))
    if report.gates["model_file_present"]:
        check_manifest(model, report)
def check_manifest(model: Path, report: Report) -> None:
    """Every tensor of the base checkpoint, with the same name, shape and dtype, and nothing else."""
    from safetensors import safe_open
    want = json.loads(MANIFEST.read_text())["tensors"]
    problems = []
    try:
        with safe_open(str(model), "pt") as f:
            keys = set(f.keys())
            for k in sorted(keys - set(want))[:5]:
                problems.append(f"unexpected tensor {k}")
            for k in sorted(set(want) - keys)[:5]:
                problems.append(f"missing tensor {k}")
            for k in keys & set(want):
                s = f.get_slice(k)
                if list(s.get_shape()) != want[k]["shape"] or s.get_dtype() != want[k]["dtype"]:
                    problems.append(f"{k}: {s.get_dtype()} {list(s.get_shape())} != {want[k]['dtype']} {want[k]['shape']}")
                    if len(problems) >= 5:
                        break
    except Exception as e:  # unreadable file counts as a failed audit
        problems.append(f"cannot read safetensors: {e}")
    report.gates["weights_match_manifest"] = not problems
    report.notes.extend(problems)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------- GPU evaluation
def model_dir(model_file: Path) -> Path:
    """A model directory: the base model's config, tokenizer and chat template, the submission's weights."""
    tmp = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="submission-"))
    for p in BASE_MODEL_DIR.iterdir():
        if not p.name.startswith("model.safetensors"):
            (tmp / p.name).symlink_to(p.resolve())
    (tmp / "model.safetensors").symlink_to(model_file.resolve())
    weight_map = {k: "model.safetensors" for k in json.loads(MANIFEST.read_text())["tensors"]}
    (tmp / "model.safetensors.index.json").write_text(json.dumps({"metadata": {}, "weight_map": weight_map}))
    return tmp
def load_model(model_file: Path):
    """The base model's config, tokenizer and chat template with only the weights swapped."""
    import torch
    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer
    tmp = model_dir(model_file)
    tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(str(tmp))
    tok.padding_side = "left"
    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(str(tmp), dtype=torch.bfloat16).to("cuda").eval()
    return model, tok
VLLM_PYTHON = os.environ.get("AR_VLLM_PYTHON", "/opt/vllm/bin/python3")
def run_gsm8k_vllm(mdir: Path, items: Path, limit: int | None, out_dir: Path | None) -> tuple[int, int]:
    """GSM8K with thinking on, sampled as the model card recommends; see eval/gsm8k_vllm.py."""
    import subprocess
    out = (out_dir or Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="gsm8k-"))) / "gsm8k.jsonl"
    cmd = [VLLM_PYTHON, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "eval" / "gsm8k_vllm.py"),
           "--model-dir", str(mdir), "--items", str(items), "--out", str(out)] + (["--limit", str(limit)] if limit else [])
    r = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True)
    if r.returncode != 0:
        raise RuntimeError(f"gsm8k_vllm.py failed ({r.returncode}):\n{r.stderr[-3000:]}")
    # vLLM logs to stdout too (its shutdown lines come after the result)
    res = next(json.loads(l) for l in reversed(r.stdout.splitlines()) if l.startswith('{"correct"'))
    return res["correct"], res["n"]
def all_finite(model) -> bool:
    import torch
    return all(torch.isfinite(p).all().item() for p in model.parameters())

    """No system prompt, thinking disabled, greedy."""
    import torch

def all_finite(model) -> bool:
    import torch
    return all(torch.isfinite(p).all().item() for p in model.parameters())
def generate(model, tok, prompts: list[str], max_new_tokens: int, batch_size: int) -> list[str]:
    """No system prompt, thinking disabled, greedy."""
    import torch
    outs: list[str] = []
    with torch.no_grad():
        for i in range(0, len(prompts), batch_size):
            texts = [tok.apply_chat_template([{"role": "user", "content": p}], tokenize=False,
                                             add_generation_prompt=True, enable_thinking=False)
                     for p in prompts[i:i + batch_size]]
            enc = tok(texts, return_tensors="pt", padding=True).to(model.device)
            gen = model.generate(**enc, max_new_tokens=max_new_tokens, do_sample=False,
                                 pad_token_id=tok.pad_token_id)
            outs += tok.batch_decode(gen[:, enc.input_ids.shape[1]:], skip_special_tokens=True)
    return outs
def _fold(s: str) -> str:
    s = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", s).encode("ascii", "ignore").decode().lower()
    return " " + " ".join(re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9 ]", " ", s).split()) + " "
def correct(kind: str, response: str, item: dict) -> bool:
    if kind == "contains":
        return any(_fold(a) in _fold(response) for a in [item["answer"], *item.get("aliases", [])])
    if kind == "letter":
        m = re.search(r"\b([ABCD])\b", response)
        return bool(m) and m.group(1) == item["answer"]
    if kind == "number":
        m = re.findall(r"Answer:\s*\$?(-?[\d,]*\.?\d+)", response)
        if not m:
            return False
        try:
            return abs(float(m[-1].replace(",", "")) - float(item["answer"])) < 1e-6
        except ValueError:
            return False
    raise ValueError(kind)
def run_benchmark(model, tok, name: str, items: list[dict], batch_size: int, out_dir: Path | None = None) -> tuple[int, int]:
    kind, max_new = BENCHMARKS[name.removeprefix(DEV)]
    prompts = [it["question"] + (GSM8K_SUFFIX if kind == "number" else "") for it in items]
    responses = generate(model, tok, prompts, max_new, batch_size)
    ok = [correct(kind, r, it) for r, it in zip(responses, items)]
    if out_dir is not None:
        out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        (out_dir / f"{name}.jsonl").write_text("".join(
            json.dumps({"id": it["id"], "response": r, "correct": o}, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n"
            for it, r, o in zip(items, responses, ok)))
    return sum(ok), len(ok)
def load_items(path: Path, limit: int | None = None) -> list[dict]:
    items = [json.loads(l) for l in path.read_text().splitlines() if l.strip()]
    return items[:limit] if limit else items
def evaluate(output_dir: Path, data: dict[str, Path], baselines: dict[str, float], sc: dict,
             responses_dir: Path | None = None) -> Report:
    """Gates, then the benchmarks, then the score. `data` maps benchmark name -> jsonl path."""
    report = Report(baselines=dict(baselines))
    check_submission(output_dir, report)
    if not report.gated:
        return report
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    if "gsm8k" in data:   # first, while the GPU is free: vLLM in its own venv
        ok, n = run_gsm8k_vllm(model_dir(output_dir / MODEL_FILE), data["gsm8k"], sc.get("n_gsm8k"), responses_dir)
        report.metrics["gsm8k"] = ok / n
        report.counts["gsm8k"] = [ok, n]
    model, tok = load_model(output_dir / MODEL_FILE)
    report.gates["weights_finite"] = all_finite(model)
    if not report.gates["weights_finite"]:
        report.notes.append("non-finite values in the weights")
        return report
    limits = {n: sc.get("n_retention") for n in BENCHMARKS if n.startswith("retention")}
    limits["gsm8k"] = sc.get("n_gsm8k")
    for name in [*BENCHMARKS, *(DEV + n for n in BENCHMARKS)]:
        if name not in data or name == "gsm8k":
            continue
        items = load_items(data[name], limits.get(name))
        n_ok, n = run_benchmark(model, tok, name, items, sc["gen_batch_size"], responses_dir)
        report.metrics[name] = n_ok / n
        report.counts[name] = [n_ok, n]
    report.eval_wall_sec = time.monotonic() - t0
    report.gates["within_eval_wall_clock"] = report.eval_wall_sec <= sc["eval_wall_sec"]
    if not report.gates["within_eval_wall_clock"]:
        report.notes.append(f"evaluation took {report.eval_wall_sec:.0f}s, budget {sc['eval_wall_sec']}s")
    return report

#16 execconst patch = "*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: /workspace/train.py\n+import json, os, random, re, time\n+from pathlib import Path\n+\n+import torch\n+import torch.nn as nn\n+import torch.nn.functional as F\n+from safetensors.torch import save_file\n+from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer\n+\n+BASE = \"/task/models/Qwen3.5-4B\"\n+OUT = Path(\"/workspace/output/model.safetensors\")\n+SEED = 20261103\n+random.seed(SEED)\n+torch.manual_seed(SEED)\n+torch.cuda.manual_seed_all(SEED)\n+torch.backends.cuda.matmul.allow_tf32 = True\n+\n+\n+def read_jsonl(path):\n+ with open(path) as f:\n+ return [json.loads(x) for x in f if x.strip()]\n+\n+\n+class LoRALinear(nn.Module):\n+ def __init__(self, base, rank=16, alpha=16):\n+ super().__init__()\n+ self.base = base\n+ self.rank = rank\n+ self.scale = alpha / rank\n+ self.A = nn.Parameter(torch.empty(rank, base.in_features, device=base.weight.device, dtype=torch.float32))\n+ self.B = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(base.out_features, rank, device=base.weight.device, dtype=torch.float32))\n+ nn.init.normal_(self.A, std=0.01)\n+\n+ def forward(self, x):\n+ y = self.base(x)\n+ # Compute the small adapter path in fp32, then return the base dtype.\n+ z = F.linear(F.linear(x.to(torch.float32), self.A), self.B) * self.scale\n+ return y + z.to(y.dtype)\n+\n+ @torch.no_grad()\n+ def merge(self):\n+ delta = (self.B @ self.A) * self.scale\n+ self.base.weight.add_(delta.to(self.base.weight.dtype))\n+ return self.base\n+\n+\n+def install_lora(model, rank=16):\n+ targets = []\n+ for name, module in list(model.named_modules()):\n+ if not isinstance(module, nn.Linear) or not name.startswith(\"model.layers.\"):\n+ continue\n+ if any(x in name for x in (\"linear_attn.\", \"self_attn.\", \".mlp.\")):\n+ targets.append(name)\n+ for name in targets:\n+ parent_name, child = name.rsplit(\".\", 1)\n+ parent = model.get_submodule(parent_name)\n+ setattr(parent, child, LoRALinear(getattr(parent, child), rank=rank))\n+ return len(targets)\n+\n+\n+def chat_ids(tok, question, answer):\n+ user = [{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": question}]\n+ full = [{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": question}, {\"role\": \"assistant\", \"content\": answer}]\n+ p = tok.apply_chat_template(user, tokenize=True, add_generation_prompt=True, enable_thinking=False)\n+ ids = tok.apply_chat_template(full, tokenize=True, add_generation_prompt=False, enable_thinking=False)\n+ if ids[:len(p)] != p:\n+ # Tokenization can differ at the assistant boundary; find the common prefix.\n+ n = 0\n+ while n < min(len(p), len(ids)) and p[n] == ids[n]:\n+ n += 1\n+ plen = n\n+ else:\n+ plen = len(p)\n+ if plen >= len(ids):\n+ return None\n+ return ids[:768], plen\n+\n+\n+def clean_paraphrases(response, answer):\n+ out = []\n+ answer_fold = re.sub(r\"[^a-z0-9]\", \"\", answer.lower())\n+ for line in response.splitlines():\n+ line = re.sub(r\"^\\s*(?:[-*•]|\\d+[.)])\\s*\", \"\", line).strip().strip('\"')\n+ line = re.sub(r\"^(?:Paraphrase|Question)\\s*:\\s*\", \"\", line, flags=re.I).strip()\n+ if len(line) < 12 or len(line) > 220:\n+ continue\n+ if answer_fold and answer_fold in re.sub(r\"[^a-z0-9]\", \"\", line.lower()):\n+ continue\n+ if \"?\" not in line and not re.match(r\"(?i)^(?:who|what|when|where|which|how|name|identify|tell me)\", line):\n+ continue\n+ out.append(line)\n+ # De-duplicate and retain at most three generated variants.\n+ unique = []\n+ for s in out:\n+ if s.lower() not in [x.lower() for x in unique]:\n+ unique.append(s)\n+ return unique[:3]\n+\n+\n+def make_examples(model, tok):\n+ facts = (read_jsonl(\"/task/public/facts.jsonl\") +\n+ read_jsonl(\"/task/public/astro_facts.jsonl\") +\n+ read_jsonl(\"/task/public/news_facts.jsonl\"))\n+ paraphrases = read_jsonl(\"/task/public/paraphrases.jsonl\")\n+ all_qas = [(x[\"question\"], x[\"answer\"], x) for x in facts]\n+ all_qas.extend((x[\"question\"], x[\"answer\"], x) for x in paraphrases)\n+\n+ # Ask the base model for meaning-preserving variants, conditioned on the answer\n+ # only while creating training text. Variants that reveal the answer are filtered.\n+ paraprompt = []\n+ for q, a, _ in facts:\n+ text = (f\"Rewrite the factual question below in three different natural ways. \"\n+ f\"Preserve exactly what it asks and do not answer or mention the answer. \"\n+ f\"The answer, for disambiguation only, is: {a}\\nQuestion: {q}\\n\"\n+ \"Return only the three rewritten questions, one per line.\")\n+ paraprompt.append(tok.apply_chat_template([{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": text}],\n+ tokenize=False, add_generation_prompt=True, enable_thinking=False))\n+ variants = {}\n+ print(f\"Generating paraphrases for {len(paraprompt)} facts\", flush=True)\n+ with torch.inference_mode():\n+ for start in range(0, len(paraprompt), 16):\n+ enc = tok(paraprompt[start:start+16], return_tensors=\"pt\", padding=True,\n+ truncation=True, max_length=512).to(model.device)\n+ gen = model.generate(**enc, max_new_tokens=144, do_sample=True, temperature=0.72,\n+ top_p=0.9, pad_token_id=tok.pad_token_id,\n+ eos_token_id=tok.eos_token_id)\n+ texts = tok.batch_decode(gen[:, enc.input_ids.shape[1]:], skip_special_tokens=True)\n+ for j, txt in enumerate(texts):\n+ fi = start + j\n+ variants[facts[fi][\"id\"]] = clean_paraphrases(txt, facts[fi][\"answer\"])\n+ print(\"Generated variants retained:\", sum(map(len, variants.values())), flush=True)\n+\n+ examples = []\n+ # Add one direct example, generated paraphrases, and two light conversational rewrites\n+ # per fact. The answers stay short so the model retrieves the entity immediately.\n+ for q, a, fact in all_qas:\n+ qs = [q]\n+ qs += variants.get(fact[\"id\"], []) if fact[\"id\"] in variants else []\n+ if fact not in paraphrases:\n+ qs += [\"Can you tell me \" + q[0].lower() + q[1:] if q.endswith(\"?\") else q,\n+ \"Please answer this question: \" + q]\n+ for qq in dict.fromkeys(qs):\n+ examples.append((qq, a))\n+\n+ # Add evidence-conditioned retrieval examples. Their context is extracted from\n+ # the matching source page and is never required at evaluation time.\n+ corpus = {}\n+ for fn in (\"astro_corpus.jsonl\", \"news_corpus.jsonl\"):\n+ for page in read_jsonl(\"/task/public/\" + fn):\n+ corpus[page.get(\"url\", \"\")] = page.get(\"text\", \"\")\n+ evidence_n = 0\n+ for fact in facts:\n+ url = fact.get(\"source\")\n+ text = corpus.get(url, \"\")\n+ if not text:\n+ continue\n+ answer = fact[\"answer\"]\n+ idx = text.lower().find(answer.lower())\n+ if idx < 0:\n+ # Try aliases, but only use a real source mention.\n+ for alias in fact.get(\"aliases\", []):\n+ if len(alias) > 2 and alias.lower() in text.lower():\n+ idx = text.lower().find(alias.lower())\n+ break\n+ if idx < 0:\n+ continue\n+ lo, hi = max(0, idx - 220), min(len(text), idx + len(answer) + 240)\n+ excerpt = \" \".join(text[lo:hi].split())\n+ examples.append((f\"Use this report excerpt to answer the question.\\n\\n{excerpt}\\n\\nQuestion: {fact['question']}\", answer))\n+ evidence_n += 1\n+ print(f\"Added {evidence_n} evidence-conditioned examples\", flush=True)\n+\n+ # Multiple-choice training teaches the model to select robustly when probes include\n+ # lettered options; open-ended questions still have separate answer-only examples.\n+ groups = {}\n+ for fact in facts:\n+ groups.setdefault(fact.get(\"item\", \"other\"), []).append(fact)\n+ for fact in facts:\n+ pool = groups.get(fact.get(\"item\", \"other\"), facts)\n+ answer = fact[\"answer\"]\n+ norm = lambda s: re.sub(r\"[^a-z0-9]\", \"\", s.casefold())\n+ distractors = []\n+ for candidate in pool:\n+ cand = candidate[\"answer\"]\n+ if candidate[\"id\"] != fact[\"id\"] and norm(cand) != norm(answer) and cand not in distractors:\n+ distractors.append(cand)\n+ if len(distractors) < 3:\n+ for candidate in facts:\n+ cand = candidate[\"answer\"]\n+ if candidate[\"id\"] != fact[\"id\"] and norm(cand) != norm(answer) and cand not in distractors:\n+ distractors.append(cand)\n+ if len(distractors) >= 3:\n+ break\n+ opts = random.sample(distractors, 3) + [answer]\n+ random.shuffle(opts)\n+ letter = \"ABCD\"[opts.index(answer)]\n+ options = \"\\n\".join(f\"{'ABCD'[i]}. {v}\" for i, v in enumerate(opts))\n+ examples.append((f\"{fact['question']}\\n{options}\\nChoose the correct option.\", letter))\n+\n+ # Include an explicit declarative form for each association as a second target style.\n+ for fact in facts:\n+ examples.append((fact[\"question\"], f\"The answer is {fact['answer']}.\"))\n+\n+ random.shuffle(examples)\n+ # Tokenize once; train only on assistant completion tokens.\n+ tokenized = []\n+ for q, a in examples:\n+ ex = chat_ids(tok, q, a)\n+ if ex is not None:\n+ ids, plen = ex\n+ tokenized.append((ids, plen))\n+ print(f\"Training examples: {len(tokenized)}\", flush=True)\n+ return tokenized\n+\n+\n+def batchify(batch, pad_id, device):\n+ length = max(len(x[0]) for x in batch)\n+ input_ids = torch.full((len(batch), length), pad_id, dtype=torch.long)\n+ attention = torch.zeros_like(input_ids)\n+ labels = torch.full_like(input_ids, -100)\n+ for i, (ids, plen) in enumerate(batch):\n+ n = len(ids)\n+ input_ids[i, :n] = torch.tensor(ids, dtype=torch.long)\n+ attention[i, :n] = 1\n+ labels[i, plen:n] = torch.tensor(ids[plen:n], dtype=torch.long)\n+ return {\"input_ids\": input_ids.to(device), \"attention_mask\": attention.to(device), \"labels\": labels.to(device)}\n+\n+\n+def train(model, tok, data, epochs=3, batch_size=4):\n+ lora_params = [p for p in model.parameters() if p.requires_grad]\n+ print(f\"Trainable adapter parameters: {sum(p.numel() for p in lora_params):,}\", flush=True)\n+ opt = torch.optim.AdamW(lora_params, lr=1.2e-4, betas=(0.9, 0.95), weight_decay=0.01)\n+ steps = (len(data) + batch_size - 1) // batch_size\n+ total_steps = steps * epochs\n+ step = 0\n+ for epoch in range(epochs):\n+ random.shuffle(data)\n+ total_loss = 0.0\n+ for start in range(0, len(data), batch_size):\n+ batch = batchify(data[start:start+batch_size], tok.pad_token_id, model.device)\n+ # Gentle warm-up and decay avoid large adapter drift from the pretrained model.\n+ warm = min(1.0, (step + 1) / max(30, int(total_steps * 0.03)))\n+ decay = max(0.25, 1.0 - step / max(1, total_steps))\n+ lr = 1.2e-4 * warm * decay\n+ for g in opt.param_groups:\n+ g[\"lr\"] = lr\n+ out = model(**batch, use_cache=False)\n+ loss = out.loss\n+ loss.backward()\n+ torch.nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(lora_params, 1.0)\n+ opt.step()\n+ opt.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)\n+ total_loss += float(loss.detach())\n+ step += 1\n+ if step % 100 == 0:\n+ print(f\"epoch={epoch+1}/{epochs} step={step}/{total_steps} loss={total_loss/100:.4f} lr={lr:.2g}\", flush=True)\n+ total_loss = 0.0\n+ print(f\"finished epoch {epoch+1}/{epochs}\", flush=True)\n+\n+\n+def main():\n+ print(\"Loading tokenizer/model\", flush=True)\n+ tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(BASE)\n+ model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(BASE, dtype=torch.bfloat16,\n+ attn_implementation=\"sdpa\").to(\"cuda\")\n+ model.config.use_cache = False\n+ model.eval()\n+ for p in model.parameters():\n+ p.requires_grad_(False)\n+ data = make_examples(model, tok)\n+ n_modules = install_lora(model, rank=16)\n+ model.train()\n+ print(f\"Installed rank-16 LoRA on {n_modules} text projections\", flush=True)\n+ train(model, tok, data, epochs=3, batch_size=4)\n+ model.eval()\n+ # Merge each low-rank delta into its original BF16 linear tensor.\n+ merged = 0\n+ for name, module in list(model.named_modules()):\n+ if isinstance(module, LoRALinear):\n+ parent_name, child = name.rsplit(\".\", 1)\n+ parent = model.get_submodule(parent_name)\n+ setattr(parent, child, module.merge())\n+ merged += 1\n+ print(f\"Merged {merged} adapters; exporting\", flush=True)\n+ sys_path = \"/task/eval\"\n+ import sys\n+ sys.path.insert(0, sys_path)\n+ from export import export_submission\n+ OUT.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n+ export_submission(model, OUT)\n+ print(\"Saved\", OUT, OUT.stat().st_size, flush=True)\n+\n+\n+if __name__ == \"__main__\":\n+ main()\n*** End Patch";
const patch = "*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: /workspace/train.py\n+import json, os, random, re, time\n+from pathlib import Path\n+\n+import torch\n+import torch.nn as nn\n+import torch.nn.functional as F\n+from safetensors.torch import save_file\n+from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer\n+\n+BASE = \"/task/models/Qwen3.5-4B\"\n+OUT = Path(\"/workspace/output/model.safetensors\")\n+SEED = 20261103\n+random.seed(SEED)\n+torch.manual_seed(SEED)\n+torch.cuda.manual_seed_all(SEED)\n+torch.backends.cuda.matmul.allow_tf32 = True\n+\n+\n+def read_jsonl(path):\n+    with open(path) as f:\n+        return [json.loads(x) for x in f if x.strip()]\n+\n+\n+class LoRALinear(nn.Module):\n+    def __init__(self, base, rank=16, alpha=16):\n+        super().__init__()\n+        self.base = base\n+        self.rank = rank\n+        self.scale = alpha / rank\n+        self.A = nn.Parameter(torch.empty(rank, base.in_features, device=base.weight.device, dtype=torch.float32))\n+        self.B = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(base.out_features, rank, device=base.weight.device, dtype=torch.float32))\n+        nn.init.normal_(self.A, std=0.01)\n+\n+    def forward(self, x):\n+        y = self.base(x)\n+        # Compute the small adapter path in fp32, then return the base dtype.\n+        z = F.linear(F.linear(x.to(torch.float32), self.A), self.B) * self.scale\n+        return y + z.to(y.dtype)\n+\n+    @torch.no_grad()\n+    def merge(self):\n+        delta = (self.B @ self.A) * self.scale\n+        self.base.weight.add_(delta.to(self.base.weight.dtype))\n+        return self.base\n+\n+\n+def install_lora(model, rank=16):\n+    targets = []\n+    for name, module in list(model.named_modules()):\n+        if not isinstance(module, nn.Linear) or not name.startswith(\"model.layers.\"):\n+            continue\n+        if any(x in name for x in (\"linear_attn.\", \"self_attn.\", \".mlp.\")):\n+            targets.append(name)\n+    for name in targets:\n+        parent_name, child = name.rsplit(\".\", 1)\n+        parent = model.get_submodule(parent_name)\n+        setattr(parent, child, LoRALinear(getattr(parent, child), rank=rank))\n+    return len(targets)\n+\n+\n+def chat_ids(tok, question, answer):\n+    user = [{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": question}]\n+    full = [{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": question}, {\"role\": \"assistant\", \"content\": answer}]\n+    p = tok.apply_chat_template(user, tokenize=True, add_generation_prompt=True, enable_thinking=False)\n+    ids = tok.apply_chat_template(full, tokenize=True, add_generation_prompt=False, enable_thinking=False)\n+    if ids[:len(p)] != p:\n+        # Tokenization can differ at the assistant boundary; find the common prefix.\n+        n = 0\n+        while n < min(len(p), len(ids)) and p[n] == ids[n]:\n+            n += 1\n+        plen = n\n+    else:\n+        plen = len(p)\n+    if plen >= len(ids):\n+        return None\n+    return ids[:768], plen\n+\n+\n+def clean_paraphrases(response, answer):\n+    out = []\n+    answer_fold = re.sub(r\"[^a-z0-9]\", \"\", answer.lower())\n+    for line in response.splitlines():\n+        line = re.sub(r\"^\\s*(?:[-*•]|\\d+[.)])\\s*\", \"\", line).strip().strip('\"')\n+        line = re.sub(r\"^(?:Paraphrase|Question)\\s*:\\s*\", \"\", line, flags=re.I).strip()\n+        if len(line) < 12 or len(line) > 220:\n+            continue\n+        if answer_fold and answer_fold in re.sub(r\"[^a-z0-9]\", \"\", line.lower()):\n+            continue\n+        if \"?\" not in line and not re.match(r\"(?i)^(?:who|what|when|where|which|how|name|identify|tell me)\", line):\n+            continue\n+        out.append(line)\n+    # De-duplicate and retain at most three generated variants.\n+    unique = []\n+    for s in out:\n+        if s.lower() not in [x.lower() for x in unique]:\n+            unique.append(s)\n+    return unique[:3]\n+\n+\n+def make_examples(model, tok):\n+    facts = (read_jsonl(\"/task/public/facts.jsonl\") +\n+             read_jsonl(\"/task/public/astro_facts.jsonl\") +\n+             read_jsonl(\"/task/public/news_facts.jsonl\"))\n+    paraphrases = read_jsonl(\"/task/public/paraphrases.jsonl\")\n+    all_qas = [(x[\"question\"], x[\"answer\"], x) for x in facts]\n+    all_qas.extend((x[\"question\"], x[\"answer\"], x) for x in paraphrases)\n+\n+    # Ask the base model for meaning-preserving variants, conditioned on the answer\n+    # only while creating training text. Variants that reveal the answer are filtered.\n+    paraprompt = []\n+    for q, a, _ in facts:\n+        text = (f\"Rewrite the factual question below in three different natural ways. \"\n+                f\"Preserve exactly what it asks and do not answer or mention the answer. \"\n+                f\"The answer, for disambiguation only, is: {a}\\nQuestion: {q}\\n\"\n+                \"Return only the three rewritten questions, one per line.\")\n+        paraprompt.append(tok.apply_chat_template([{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": text}],\n+                         tokenize=False, add_generation_prompt=True, enable_thinking=False))\n+    variants = {}\n+    print(f\"Generating paraphrases for {len(paraprompt)} facts\", flush=True)\n+    with torch.inference_mode():\n+        for start in range(0, len(paraprompt), 16):\n+            enc = tok(paraprompt[start:start+16], return_tensors=\"pt\", padding=True,\n+                      truncation=True, max_length=512).to(model.device)\n+            gen = model.generate(**enc, max_new_tokens=144, do_sample=True, temperature=0.72,\n+                                 top_p=0.9, pad_token_id=tok.pad_token_id,\n+                                 eos_token_id=tok.eos_token_id)\n+            texts = tok.batch_decode(gen[:, enc.input_ids.shape[1]:], skip_special_tokens=True)\n+            for j, txt in enumerate(texts):\n+                fi = start + j\n+                variants[facts[fi][\"id\"]] = clean_paraphrases(txt, facts[fi][\"answer\"])\n+    print(\"Generated variants retained:\", sum(map(len, variants.values())), flush=True)\n+\n+    examples = []\n+    # Add one direct example, generated paraphrases, and two light conversational rewrites\n+    # per fact. The answers stay short so the model retrieves the entity immediately.\n+    for q, a, fact in all_qas:\n+        qs = [q]\n+        qs += variants.get(fact[\"id\"], []) if fact[\"id\"] in variants else []\n+        if fact not in paraphrases:\n+            qs += [\"Can you tell me \" + q[0].lower() + q[1:] if q.endswith(\"?\") else q,\n+                   \"Please answer this question: \" + q]\n+        for qq in dict.fromkeys(qs):\n+            examples.append((qq, a))\n+\n+    # Add evidence-conditioned retrieval examples. Their context is extracted from\n+    # the matching source page and is never required at evaluation time.\n+    corpus = {}\n+    for fn in (\"astro_corpus.jsonl\", \"news_corpus.jsonl\"):\n+        for page in read_jsonl(\"/task/public/\" + fn):\n+            corpus[page.get(\"url\", \"\")] = page.get(\"text\", \"\")\n+    evidence_n = 0\n+    for fact in facts:\n+        url = fact.get(\"source\")\n+        text = corpus.get(url, \"\")\n+        if not text:\n+            continue\n+        answer = fact[\"answer\"]\n+        idx = text.lower().find(answer.lower())\n+        if idx < 0:\n+            # Try aliases, but only use a real source mention.\n+            for alias in fact.get(\"aliases\", []):\n+                if len(alias) > 2 and alias.lower() in text.lower():\n+                    idx = text.lower().find(alias.lower())\n+                    break\n+        if idx < 0:\n+            continue\n+        lo, hi = max(0, idx - 220), min(len(text), idx + len(answer) + 240)\n+        excerpt = \" \".join(text[lo:hi].split())\n+        examples.append((f\"Use this report excerpt to answer the question.\\n\\n{excerpt}\\n\\nQuestion: {fact['question']}\", answer))\n+        evidence_n += 1\n+    print(f\"Added {evidence_n} evidence-conditioned examples\", flush=True)\n+\n+    # Multiple-choice training teaches the model to select robustly when probes include\n+    # lettered options; open-ended questions still have separate answer-only examples.\n+    groups = {}\n+    for fact in facts:\n+        groups.setdefault(fact.get(\"item\", \"other\"), []).append(fact)\n+    for fact in facts:\n+        pool = groups.get(fact.get(\"item\", \"other\"), facts)\n+        answer = fact[\"answer\"]\n+        norm = lambda s: re.sub(r\"[^a-z0-9]\", \"\", s.casefold())\n+        distractors = []\n+        for candidate in pool:\n+            cand = candidate[\"answer\"]\n+            if candidate[\"id\"] != fact[\"id\"] and norm(cand) != norm(answer) and cand not in distractors:\n+                distractors.append(cand)\n+        if len(distractors) < 3:\n+            for candidate in facts:\n+                cand = candidate[\"answer\"]\n+                if candidate[\"id\"] != fact[\"id\"] and norm(cand) != norm(answer) and cand not in distractors:\n+                    distractors.append(cand)\n+                if len(distractors) >= 3:\n+                    break\n+        opts = random.sample(distractors, 3) + [answer]\n+        random.shuffle(opts)\n+        letter = \"ABCD\"[opts.index(answer)]\n+        options = \"\\n\".join(f\"{'ABCD'[i]}. {v}\" for i, v in enumerate(opts))\n+        examples.append((f\"{fact['question']}\\n{options}\\nChoose the correct option.\", letter))\n+\n+    # Include an explicit declarative form for each association as a second target style.\n+    for fact in facts:\n+        examples.append((fact[\"question\"], f\"The answer is {fact['answer']}.\"))\n+\n+    random.shuffle(examples)\n+    # Tokenize once; train only on assistant completion tokens.\n+    tokenized = []\n+    for q, a in examples:\n+        ex = chat_ids(tok, q, a)\n+        if ex is not None:\n+            ids, plen = ex\n+            tokenized.append((ids, plen))\n+    print(f\"Training examples: {len(tokenized)}\", flush=True)\n+    return tokenized\n+\n+\n+def batchify(batch, pad_id, device):\n+    length = max(len(x[0]) for x in batch)\n+    input_ids = torch.full((len(batch), length), pad_id, dtype=torch.long)\n+    attention = torch.zeros_like(input_ids)\n+    labels = torch.full_like(input_ids, -100)\n+    for i, (ids, plen) in enumerate(batch):\n+        n = len(ids)\n+        input_ids[i, :n] = torch.tensor(ids, dtype=torch.long)\n+        attention[i, :n] = 1\n+        labels[i, plen:n] = torch.tensor(ids[plen:n], dtype=torch.long)\n+    return {\"input_ids\": input_ids.to(device), \"attention_mask\": attention.to(device), \"labels\": labels.to(device)}\n+\n+\n+def train(model, tok, data, epochs=3, batch_size=4):\n+    lora_params = [p for p in model.parameters() if p.requires_grad]\n+    print(f\"Trainable adapter parameters: {sum(p.numel() for p in lora_params):,}\", flush=True)\n+    opt = torch.optim.AdamW(lora_params, lr=1.2e-4, betas=(0.9, 0.95), weight_decay=0.01)\n+    steps = (len(data) + batch_size - 1) // batch_size\n+    total_steps = steps * epochs\n+    step = 0\n+    for epoch in range(epochs):\n+        random.shuffle(data)\n+        total_loss = 0.0\n+        for start in range(0, len(data), batch_size):\n+            batch = batchify(data[start:start+batch_size], tok.pad_token_id, model.device)\n+            # Gentle warm-up and decay avoid large adapter drift from the pretrained model.\n+            warm = min(1.0, (step + 1) / max(30, int(total_steps * 0.03)))\n+            decay = max(0.25, 1.0 - step / max(1, total_steps))\n+            lr = 1.2e-4 * warm * decay\n+            for g in opt.param_groups:\n+                g[\"lr\"] = lr\n+            out = model(**batch, use_cache=False)\n+            loss = out.loss\n+            loss.backward()\n+            torch.nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(lora_params, 1.0)\n+            opt.step()\n+            opt.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)\n+            total_loss += float(loss.detach())\n+            step += 1\n+            if step % 100 == 0:\n+                print(f\"epoch={epoch+1}/{epochs} step={step}/{total_steps} loss={total_loss/100:.4f} lr={lr:.2g}\", flush=True)\n+                total_loss = 0.0\n+        print(f\"finished epoch {epoch+1}/{epochs}\", flush=True)\n+\n+\n+def main():\n+    print(\"Loading tokenizer/model\", flush=True)\n+    tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(BASE)\n+    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(BASE, dtype=torch.bfloat16,\n+                                                  attn_implementation=\"sdpa\").to(\"cuda\")\n+    model.config.use_cache = False\n+    model.eval()\n+    for p in model.parameters():\n+        p.requires_grad_(False)\n+    data = make_examples(model, tok)\n+    n_modules = install_lora(model, rank=16)\n+    model.train()\n+    print(f\"Installed rank-16 LoRA on {n_modules} text projections\", flush=True)\n+    train(model, tok, data, epochs=3, batch_size=4)\n+    model.eval()\n+    # Merge each low-rank delta into its original BF16 linear tensor.\n+    merged = 0\n+    for name, module in list(model.named_modules()):\n+        if isinstance(module, LoRALinear):\n+            parent_name, child = name.rsplit(\".\", 1)\n+            parent = model.get_submodule(parent_name)\n+            setattr(parent, child, module.merge())\n+            merged += 1\n+    print(f\"Merged {merged} adapters; exporting\", flush=True)\n+    sys_path = \"/task/eval\"\n+    import sys\n+    sys.path.insert(0, sys_path)\n+    from export import export_submission\n+    OUT.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n+    export_submission(model, OUT)\n+    print(\"Saved\", OUT, OUT.stat().st_size, flush=True)\n+\n+\n+if __name__ == \"__main__\":\n+    main()\n*** End Patch";
const r = await tools.apply_patch(patch);

#!/bin/bash
# The check: the verifier's evaluation of /workspace/output/model.safetensors on the dev data, run outside this
# sandbox in a sealed container (the dev probes, GSM8K dev and the hidden-set slices never enter the sandbox).
# Writes the aggregate report to /workspace/check/report.json and prints its headline.
# - Sandbox with its own GPU (python3 /task/budget.py says so): `bash /task/check.sh`. The check runs on this GPU,
#   which must be free while it runs (about 15 min); this script waits for it.
# - Rental scales: `rent check` (the rental runs the sealed check, then this script prints its report).
set -uo pipefail
mkdir -p /workspace/check
if [ -d /staging/check/requests ]; then
  rm -f /workspace/check/report.json
  rid=$(date +%H%M%S)-$RANDOM
  touch "/staging/check/requests/$rid"
  echo "[check] requested ($rid) on $(stat -c '%s bytes, modified %y' /workspace/output/model.safetensors 2>/dev/null || echo 'no model.safetensors')"
  echo "[check] keep the GPU free until it is done (about 15 min)"
  for _ in $(seq 1 1300); do [ -f "/staging/check/done/$rid" ] && break; sleep 3; done
  [ -f "/staging/check/done/$rid" ] || { echo "[check] no result after 65 min"; exit 1; }
fi
if [ ! -f /workspace/check/report.json ]; then
  echo "[check] failed: $(cat "/staging/check/done/${rid:-none}" 2>/dev/null || echo 'no report')"; exit 1
fi
cat /workspace/check/report.json
python3 - <<'PY'
import json
r = json.load(open("/workspace/check/report.json"))
print(f"\ncheck score {r['score']:.3f}  (learning² × retention: learning {r['learning']:.3f} on the dev facts' probes; "
      f"retention {r['retention']:.3f} on GSM8K dev and the hidden-knowledge slices)  gates passed: {all(r['gates'].values())}")
raise SystemExit(0 if all(r["gates"].values()) else 1)
PY

The first check passed all file gates but shows a poor tradeoff: learning is 0.380, while retention is 0.546 (GSM8K 0.765, hidden-slice ratio 0.498). I’m going to reduce the merged update and rerun the check; the current adapter changes appear too broad for the retention target.

Ran 40 commandsexec ×40 (10m)
#161 execcp /workspace/output/model.safetensors /workspace/model_full.safetensors && cat > /workspace/scale_checkpoint.py <<'PY'
cp /workspace/output/model.safetensors /workspace/model_full.safetensors && cat > /workspace/scale_checkpoint.py <<'PY'
import json, torch
from pathlib import Path
from safetensors import safe_open
from safetensors.torch import save_file
base_dir=Path('/task/models/Qwen3.5-4B')
source=Path('/workspace/model_full.safetensors')
out=Path('/workspace/model_half.safetensors')
# Read base tensors in their exact checkpoint layout.
base={}
for shard in sorted(base_dir.glob('model.safetensors-*.safetensors')):
    with safe_open(str(shard),framework='pt',device='cpu') as f:
        for k in f.keys(): base[k]=f.get_tensor(k)
new={}
changed=[]
with safe_open(str(source),framework='pt',device='cpu') as f:
    for k in f.keys():
        cur=f.get_tensor(k)
        b=base[k]
        if torch.equal(cur,b):
            new[k]=cur
        else:
            # Scale the learned weight delta toward the original checkpoint.
            new[k]=(b.float()+0.5*(cur.float()-b.float())).to(cur.dtype).contiguous()
            changed.append(k)
print('interpolated tensors',len(changed),flush=True)
save_file(new,str(out),metadata={'format':'pt'})
print('saved',out.stat().st_size,flush=True)
PY
python3 /workspace/scale_checkpoint.py && mv /workspace/model_half.safetensors /workspace/output/model.safetensors && ls -lh /workspace/output

y = self.base(x)
        # Compute the small adapter path in fp32, then return the base dtype.
        z = F.linear(F.linear(x.to(torch.float32), self.A), self.B) * self.scale
        return y + z.to(y.dtype)
    @torch.no_grad()
    def merge(self):
        delta = (self.B @ self.A) * self.scale
        self.base.weight.add_(delta.to(self.base.weight.dtype))
        return self.base
def install_lora(model, rank=16):
    targets = []
    for name, module in list(model.named_modules()):
        if not isinstance(module, nn.Linear) or not name.startswith("model.layers."):
            continue
        layer_index = int(name.split(".")[2])
        # Keep the pretrained lower and middle feature extractor unchanged; adapt
        # only the final twelve text blocks for fact retrieval.
        if layer_index < 20:
            continue
        if any(x in name for x in ("linear_attn.", "self_attn.", ".mlp.")):
            targets.append(name)
    for name in targets:
        parent_name, child = name.rsplit(".", 1)
        parent = model.get_submodule(parent_name)
        setattr(parent, child, LoRALinear(getattr(parent, child), rank=rank))
    return len(targets)
def chat_ids(tok, question, answer):
    user = [{"role": "user", "content": question}]
    full = [{"role": "user", "content": question}, {"role": "assistant", "content": answer}]
    p = tok.apply_chat_template(user, tokenize=True, add_generation_prompt=True, enable_thinking=False)
    ids = tok.apply_chat_template(full, tokenize=True, add_generation_prompt=False, enable_thinking=False)
    if hasattr(p, "input_ids"):
        p = p.input_ids
    if hasattr(ids, "input_ids"):
        ids = ids.input_ids
    if ids[:len(p)] != p:
        # Tokenization can differ at the assistant boundary; find the common prefix.
        n = 0
        while n < min(len(p), len(ids)) and p[n] == ids[n]:
            n += 1
        plen = n
    else:
        plen = len(p)
    if plen >= len(ids):
        return None
    return ids[:768], plen

                          truncation=True, max_length=512).to(model.device)

                                     top_p=0.9, pad_token_id=tok.pad_token_id,
                                     eos_token_id=tok.eos_token_id)

                for j, txt in enumerate(texts):
                    fi = start + j
                    variants[facts[fi]["id"]] = clean_paraphrases(txt, facts[fi]["answer"])
        cache.write_text(json.dumps(variants, ensure_ascii=False))
    else:
        print("Loaded cached paraphrases", flush=True)
    print("Generated variants retained:", sum(map(len, variants.values())), flush=True)
    # Generate one declarative surface for each association and train a cloze
    # retrieval form as well. This broadens the relation representation beyond
    # matching the interrogative syntax used in the public fact files.

                      "Use only the supplied fact; do not add another claim.\n"

            statement_prompts.append(tok.apply_chat_template([{"role": "user", "content": prompt}],
                                    tokenize=False, add_generation_prompt=True, enable_thinking=False))
        print(f"Generating declarative forms for {len(facts)} facts", flush=True)

            for start in range(0, len(statement_prompts), 16):

                          truncation=True, max_length=512).to(model.device)
                gen = model.generate(**enc, max_new_tokens=80, do_sample=False,
                                     pad_token_id=tok.pad_token_id, eos_token_id=tok.eos_token_id)
                texts = tok.batch_decode(gen[:, enc.input_ids.shape[1]:], skip_special_tokens=True)
                for j, txt in enumerate(texts):
                    statements[facts[start+j]["id"]] = " ".join(txt.split()).strip()
        statement_cache.write_text(json.dumps(statements, ensure_ascii=False))
    else:
        print("Loaded cached declarative forms", flush=True)

    # Add one direct example, generated paraphrases, and two light conversational rewrites
    # per fact. The answers stay short so the model retrieves the entity immediately.
    for q, a, fact in all_qas:
        qs = [q]
        qs += variants.get(fact["id"], []) if fact["id"] in variants else []
        if fact not in paraphrases:

                   "Please answer this question: " + q]
        for qq in dict.fromkeys(qs):
            examples.append((qq, a))
    # Add evidence-conditioned retrieval examples. Their context is extracted from
    # the matching source page and is never required at evaluation time.
    corpus = {}
    for fn in ("astro_corpus.jsonl", "news_corpus.jsonl"):
        for page in read_jsonl("/task/public/" + fn):
            corpus[page.get("url", "")] = page.get("text", "")
    evidence_n = 0
    for fact in facts:
        url = fact.get("source")
        text = corpus.get(url, "")
        if not text:
            continue
        answer = fact["answer"]
        idx = text.lower().find(answer.lower())
        if idx < 0:
            # Try aliases, but only use a real source mention.
            for alias in fact.get("aliases", []):
                if len(alias) > 2 and alias.lower() in text.lower():
                    idx = text.lower().find(alias.lower())
                    break
        if idx < 0:
            continue
        lo, hi = max(0, idx - 220), min(len(text), idx + len(answer) + 240)
        excerpt = " ".join(text[lo:hi].split())
        examples.append((f"Use this report excerpt to answer the question.\n\n{excerpt}\n\nQuestion: {fact['question']}", answer))
        evidence_n += 1
    print(f"Added {evidence_n} evidence-conditioned examples", flush=True)
    # Multiple-choice training teaches the model to select robustly when probes include
    # lettered options; open-ended questions still have separate answer-only examples.
    groups = {}
    for fact in facts:
        groups.setdefault(fact.get("item", "other"), []).append(fact)
    for fact in facts:
        pool = groups.get(fact.get("item", "other"), facts)
        answer = fact["answer"]
        norm = lambda s: re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]", "", s.casefold())
        distractors = []
        for candidate in pool:
            cand = candidate["answer"]
            if candidate["id"] != fact["id"] and norm(cand) != norm(answer) and cand not in distractors:
                distractors.append(cand)
        if len(distractors) < 3:
            for candidate in facts:
                cand = candidate["answer"]
                if candidate["id"] != fact["id"] and norm(cand) != norm(answer) and cand not in distractors:
                    distractors.append(cand)
                if len(distractors) >= 3:
                    break
                total_loss = 0.0
        print(f"finished epoch {epoch+1}/{epochs}", flush=True)
def main():
    print("Loading tokenizer/model", flush=True)
    tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(BASE)
    tok.padding_side = "left"
    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(BASE, dtype=torch.bfloat16,
                                                  attn_implementation="sdpa").to("cuda")
    model.config.use_cache = False
    model.eval()
    for p in model.parameters():
        p.requires_grad_(False)
    data = make_examples(model, tok)
    n_modules = install_lora(model, rank=16)
    model.train()
    print(f"Installed rank-16 LoRA on {n_modules} text projections", flush=True)
    train(model, tok, data, epochs=2, batch_size=4)
    model.eval()
    # Merge each low-rank delta into its original BF16 linear tensor.
    merged = 0
    for name, module in list(model.named_modules()):
        if isinstance(module, LoRALinear):
            parent_name, child = name.rsplit(".", 1)
            parent = model.get_submodule(parent_name)
            setattr(parent, child, module.merge())
            merged += 1
    print(f"Merged {merged} adapters; exporting", flush=True)
    sys_path = "/task/eval"
    import sys
    sys.path.insert(0, sys_path)
    from export import export_submission
    OUT.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    export_submission(model, OUT)
    print("Saved", OUT, OUT.stat().st_size, flush=True)
if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

# Multiple-choice training teaches the model to select robustly when probes include
    # lettered options; open-ended questions still have separate answer-only examples.
    groups = {}
    for fact in facts:
        groups.setdefault(fact.get("item", "other"), []).append(fact)
    for fact in facts:
        pool = groups.get(fact.get("item", "other"), facts)
        answer = fact["answer"]
        norm = lambda s: re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]", "", s.casefold())
        distractors = []
        for candidate in pool:
            cand = candidate["answer"]
            if candidate["id"] != fact["id"] and norm(cand) != norm(answer) and cand not in distractors:
                distractors.append(cand)
        if len(distractors) < 3:
            for candidate in facts:
                cand = candidate["answer"]
                if candidate["id"] != fact["id"] and norm(cand) != norm(answer) and cand not in distractors:
                    distractors.append(cand)
                if len(distractors) >= 3:
                    break
        opts = random.sample(distractors, 3) + [answer]
        random.shuffle(opts)
        letter = "ABCD"[opts.index(answer)]
        options = "\n".join(f"{'ABCD'[i]}. {v}" for i, v in enumerate(opts))
        examples.append((f"{fact['question']}\n{options}\nChoose the correct option.", letter))
    # Include an explicit declarative form for each association as a second target style.
    for fact in facts:
        examples.append((fact["question"], f"The answer is {fact['answer']}."))
    def norm(s):
        s = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", s).encode("ascii", "ignore").decode().casefold()
        return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]", "", s)
    cloze_n = 0
    for fact in facts:
        statement = statements.get(fact["id"], "")
        if not statement or len(statement) > 320:
            continue
        surfaces = sorted([fact["answer"], *fact.get("aliases", [])], key=len, reverse=True)
        surface = next((s for s in surfaces if norm(s) and norm(s) in norm(statement)), None)
        if surface is None:
            continue
        pattern = re.compile(re.escape(surface), re.I)
        clozed = pattern.sub("____", statement, count=1)
        examples.append(("Complete the statement with the missing fact.\n" + clozed, fact["answer"]))
        cloze_n += 1
    print(f"Added {cloze_n} declarative cloze examples", flush=True)
    random.shuffle(examples)
    # Tokenize once; train only on assistant completion tokens.
    tokenized = []
    for q, a in examples:
        ex = chat_ids(tok, q, a)
        if ex is not None:
            ids, plen = ex
            tokenized.append((ids, plen))
    print(f"Training examples: {len(tokenized)}", flush=True)
    return tokenized
